  ### Interface que F3a e F3b devem usar (`src/ui/views/registry.ts`)
  ```ts
  interface View {
    readonly id: string;
    titulo(state: State): string;
    readonly dwell: number;                 // segundos
    needs(state: State): Need[];            // a casca busca e reassina pelo SSE
    mount(el: HTMLElement): void;
    update(state: State, diff: Diff): void; // chamado após mount e a cada mudança
    unmount(): void;
    keys?(tecla: string, state: State): boolean; // ↑/↓; true = tratou
  }
  register(id: string, fabrica: () => View, rotulo?: string); get(id); has(id); listar();
  partes(el) → { titulo, chips, corpo, painel }; placeholder(id, titulo | (s)=>string)
  type Need = {tipo:"resultado",ele,cargo,abr} | {tipo:"mapa",ele,cargo,nivel:"uf"|"mun"|"zona",pai}
            | {tipo:"serie",ele,cargo,abr} | {tipo:"estado"} | {tipo:"anomalias"}
  ```
  - **Esqueleto:** a casca cria `section.tela > .palco(.palco-cab > h1.palco-titulo + .palco-chips, .palco-corpo) + aside.painel` e escreve o h1 a partir de `titulo(s)`. A tela preenche `.palco-corpo`, `.palco-chips` e `.painel`; a classe `tela--cheia` no `el` ocupa a largura toda.
  - **Instâncias:** cada troca de tela, UF, município ou zona monta uma instância nova, com crossfade de 350 ms.
  - **Onde registrar:** em `src/ui/views/index.ts`, trocando o placeholder por `register("pres", criarPresidente, ROTULOS.pres)`. A última chamada vence. Os títulos prontos estão em `tituloDaTela(id, s, uf, mun)`.
  
  ### API do store (`state/store.ts`)
  - `get()` devolve o estado atual.
  - `subscribe((s, diff) => …)` devolve uma função para cancelar. As notificações saem agrupadas numa microtarefa.
  - `patch(caminho, valor)` recebe caminho com pontos ou array e faz cópia estrutural, então ramos intocados mantêm a mesma referência. Devolve um `Diff` de `{path, de, para}` com as folhas numéricas que mudaram; arrays são casados por `sqcand|uf|cd|sg`, por exemplo `resultados.6257:1:br.cand[sqcand=…].vap`.
  - `ui(parcial)` mescla campos do UiState.
  - O estado tem `config`, `estado`, `resultados`, `mapas`, `series`, `anomalias`, `campos`, `cores`, `playlist`, `rede` e `ui`. As chaves vêm de `chaveResultado(ele,cargo,abr)`, `chaveMapa(ele,cargo,nivel,pai)` e `chaveSerie`.
  
  ### Peças que F2, F3a e F3b podem reaproveitar
  - **Cores** (`data/cores.ts`): `toLab`, `fromLab`, `mix`, `ramp`, `banda(pst)` (0, 0,35, 0,60, 0,85 ou 1), `corPorBanda(cor, pst)` (cinza `#d9d6cc` quando 0), `corCandidato(n, pos, cores)`, `corCampo(campo, campos)`, `contraste`, `textoSobre`.
  - **Seletores:** `ranking`, `disputa` (líder, segundo, margem; empate sem líder), `agregarPorCampo`, `camposDosCandidatos`, `composicaoVotos`, `colapsarOutros` + `ehOutros`/`rotuloOutros`, `ufsPorEleitorado`, `estadoUfsOrdenado`, `apuracaoComecou`.
  - **Formatação:** `inteiro`, `pct(x, 1|2)` (x já em pontos), `comSinal`, `compacto`, `eleitores` ("660 mil eleitores", "2,01 mi de eleitores"), `hora` e `horaCurta` (Brasília), `duracao`, `dezessete`, `nomeProprio`.
  - **Componentes:** `chip`, `chipAndamento(pst, tf)`, `chipSituacao(st, e, dvt)`, `foto({sqcand, nome, tamanho, cor})` com monograma quando falta a foto, e a classe CSS `.mudou` para o sublinhado lime.
  - **Malhas, para F2** (`data/geo.ts`): `malhaUfs()` lê `/geo/br_uf.geojson`, `malhaMunicipios(uf)` lê `/geo/mun/{UF}.geojson`, mais `preCarregar` e `codigoIbge(feicao)`.
  
  ### Pontos em que fixei ou mudei o contrato
  1. **Datas e flags:** horários são strings ISO ou `null`; `tf` e `cand.e` são booleanos; `st` e `dvt` continuam texto do TSE. O servidor (B3) precisa converter "s"/"n".
  2. **Caixa:** UFs em caixa alta em `config.municipios`, `ui.uf` e `estado.ufs` (a comparação ignora caixa). `abr` segue o TSE em minúsculas: `abrDe(uf, mun, zona)` dá "br", "sp", "sp71072" ou "sp71072-z0248". `pai` do mapa usa a mesma forma ("br", "sp", "sp71072").
  3. **Mapa:** `lider`, `segundo` e `top[]` têm o formato `LiderUnidade {sqcand,n,nmu,sg,campo,vap,pvapn}`.
  4. **Hash `z`:** no UiState virou `zonas: boolean` mais `zona: string | null`. `z=1` liga as zonas sem escolher zona; `z=0248` escolhe a zona. Também entram no hash `dwell=` e `m=`.
  5. **Playlist:** `normalizarPlaylist` aceita o formato do F0 (`view` em vez de `v`, e lista `destaque` como ordem de prioridade das UFs). `$destaque` pula UFs com pst menor que 5 e só limita a quantidade se `max` for dado. Com a playlist atual, o ciclo tem 23 telas.
  6. **Ativos do F0:** `cores.json` (`candidatos`, `terceiro`, `outros`) e `campos.json` (`campos{cor,rotulo}`, `partidos`) são normalizados com valores padrão. As fotos ficam em `/fotos/{sqcand}.jpg`, o mesmo que o F0 gerou (20.063 arquivos).
  7. **Contraste:** o teal `#0f7f5f` dá 4,37:1 sobre o papel, abaixo de AA para texto pequeno. Acrescentei `--outros-texto` / `OUTROS_TEXTO` `#0b6e55` (5,47:1); o teal original fica para barra, mapa e texto grande.
  8. **Letras de comando que começam UF:** `a`, `m` e `r` esperam a janela de 900 ms; se a segunda letra fechar uma UF, vale a UF.
  
  ### Mock
  - **Relógio:** simulado, de 16:59:30 a cerca de 00:00 de 04/10, a `speed` vezes a velocidade real.
  - **Presidente:** os 12 candidatos reais do arquivo do TSE, com viradas forçadas em MG (para Flávio) e AM (para Lula) e regressão de `st` na BA entre 18:40 e 18:46.
  - **Outros cargos:** candidaturas sintéticas, chamadas "CANDIDATURA n UF".
  - **Escopo:** implementa todos os endpoints e os eventos. Usa a lista real de municípios quando `/api/config` responde; senão, uma amostra embutida com 27 UFs e 4 municípios cada.

  Three optional fields were added to `MapaOpcoes`; nothing existing changed:
  - `aoSemGeometria?(us: UnidadeMapa[])`: called when the set of units with no polygon changes.
  - `largura?` and `altura?`: the drawing box, default 1152×800.
  
  How `rotulo` is read now differs by level:
  - **uf**: the sigla is drawn by default. A non-empty string from `rotulo` replaces it.
  - **mun**: only units for which `rotulo` returns text get a label (dot, white rounded box with a thin border, measured with `getComputedTextLength` and re-measured on `document.fonts.ready`).
  - **zona**: `rotulo` sets the tile's second line. Without it the line is the leader's name and %, or just the % when the name doesn't fit.
  
  `destacar(cd)` takes `UnidadeMapa.cd`. At UF level the lowercase sigla also works. Callers should title-case municipality names themselves; the harness turns "SÃO JOSÉ DOS CAMPOS" into "São José Dos Campos", which is wrong for "dos".
  
  **Component behaviour**
  - An empty `<svg>` is added at once. An `update` that arrives before the mesh is held and applied once the mesh loads.
  - Colours are set with `style.fill` so the 600 ms CSS transition runs (0 with reduced motion). Units without data stay `#d9d6cc`.
  - Each unit gets a `<title>`.
  - Clicks are handled by one listener on the svg. The pointer cursor only appears when `aoClicar` exists.
  - `destacar` raises the node to the end of its group and draws an overlay layer: a lime 5px stroke with a 1px ink line in the middle. One SVG path can't carry two strokes, which is why it's an overlay.
  - If the mesh fails to load, the map shows "malha indisponível" and sets `data-erro`; nothing throws.
  - The svg gets `data-render-ms` and `data-pronto` attributes for QA.
  - UF labels: Archivo 600 at 24px. White text with a dark halo goes on dark fills (chosen by `textoSobre`). RN, PB, PE, AL, SE, ES and RJ keep the house `FORA` offsets, and DF too, with a leader line and a white box. An `afastarRotulos` pass stops them overlapping, since 24px labels are proportionally larger than the house's 12.5px. 72px is reserved on the right for these labels.
  - Zones: `treemapSquarify` (that is the actual d3 export name, not `treemapSquarified`), inner padding 4, tiles ordered by `cd` with numeric collation.
    - Area follows `te`. With no `te` at all, tiles are equal. A missing `te` gets the median of the ones present.
    - Labels: "Zona 0248" plus a % line from 140×70; only the number from 40×24, and only when the number fits (about 61px wide); nothing smaller.
    - The layout is only recomputed when the set of `cd`/`te` changes; otherwise tiles are just recoloured.
    - A one-zone municipality gets one full tile with the note "este município tem uma única zona".
  
  **UF code to sigla table**
  `IBGE_UF` in `mapa.ts`, copied from `scripts/voto_util_mapa.py`. It maps the `br_uf` `codarea` to the sigla; the matching key is the lowercase sigla, which equals TSE `cd`. Municipalities match `codarea` against `UnidadeMapa.cdi`.
  
  **Units missing from the mesh**
  `semGeometria()` lists the TSE units that have no polygon. They go into the svg's `data-sem-geometria` attribute (space-separated `cd`) and to `aoSemGeometria`. Tested on MT: `data-sem-geometria="73709"` (Boa Esperança do Norte, cdi 5101837), with no exception. Mesh polygons with no TSE data stay grey.

  **Desempenho de `/api/mapa?nivel=mun&pai=sp`** no banco semeado (645 municípios × 12 candidatos): 10 a 17 ms na primeira chamada e 0,3 a 0,4 ms em cache. São duas consultas: uma das unidades com a última versão até `at`, outra dos mais votados por janela `ROW_NUMBER`. O cache dura 5 s e se renova quando `max(snapshot.id)` muda.
  
  **Semântica de `at=`**
  - Qualquer ISO serve (`-03:00` também); é normalizado para UTC com milissegundos. "Até `at`" significa a última versão não regressiva com `capturado_em <= at`.
  - Vale em resultado, mapa, estado (onde `agora` passa a ser `at`), série, anomalias, snapshots, latência, fim, velocidade, andamento e fetches. ISO inválido dá 400.
  - `/api/resultado` aceita também `snapshot_id`, e responde 404 se a versão não for daquele arquivo.
  
  **Onde o formato se afasta do plano**
  - **Divergências com os tipos do F1** (`src/ui/state/types.ts`), que a integração precisa resolver:
    - Segui o seu pedido e devolvo `ufs[].uf` em minúscula ("sp"); os tipos do F1 esperam caixa alta.
    - O SSE manda `event: snapshot`, `event: evento` (com `kind:"anomalia"`) e `event: estado`. O `sse.ts` do F1 só escuta `resultado`, `estado` e `anomalia`, então precisa ganhar ouvintes para `snapshot` e `evento`.
    - O `kind` de snapshot pode ser `"ab"` ou `"config"`, valores que o tipo do F1 não prevê.
  - **Identificadores e booleanos:**
    - `eleicoes` sai como número (`{federal:6257, estadual:6259}`), não string, para combinar com o F1 e com "números como números".
    - `sqcand` e `n` saem como string, como o F1 espera.
    - `e` e `tf` saem booleanos.
  - **Campos a mais:**
    - resultado: `partidos[].tvtl` (legenda, útil nos proporcionais).
    - mapa: `snapshot_id` por unidade; `lider` e `segundo` trazem o objeto completo, com `vap`.
    - série: `pontos[].snapshot_id`, `viradas[].snapshot_id` e uma lista `candidatos`; aceita `top` (padrão 12).
    - anomalias: `categoria` (virada, regressao, fechou, atraso, outro), `abr`, `cargo`, `zona_cd` e `derivado`.
    - estado: `br.ultima_leitura_em`, `br.idade_s` e `ufs[].nome`.
  - **`top` do mapa:** só aparece quando há mais de uma vaga e é cortado em `top=` (padrão 10, máximo 100). "nv primeiros" para deputado em SP dariam 70 × 645 itens, cerca de 3 MB.
  - **`atraso_s` no estado:** é `lido_em − hg`, a nossa latência de captura. A idade do arquivo vai em `idade_s`.
  - **Nomes de município e escopo** vêm em caixa normal no servidor ("Santa Bárbara d'Oeste").
  - **`/api/velocidade`:** a taxa vai do primeiro ponto dentro da janela até o último; se só o último cair na janela, usa o anterior. A base de tempo é a hora de geração do TSE (`gerado_em`).
  - **Formatos das rotas de auditoria:**
    - `/api/fim` devolve `{total_fechados, pendentes, fechados:[…]}`.
    - `/api/latencia` devolve `{nivel, escopos:[{abr, nome, captura:{n,p50,p95,max}, geracao}], geral}`.
    - `/api/snapshots` repete as fórmulas de `v_snapshot_delta`, mas filtradas pelo arquivo, para não calcular a janela sobre o banco inteiro.
  - **Candidatos não normalizados:** quando o snapshot não tem `voto_candidato` (proporcional em município ou zona fora da primeira e da última versão), o servidor descomprime o blob com `gunzipTexto`, passa por `normalizarU(sempre)` e guarda até 32 snapshots em cache. `candidatos_normalizados:false` sinaliza esse caminho.
  - **Viradas e fechamentos derivados:** se o coletor ainda não gravou eventos `virada`, eles saem das séries de presidente (br e UF) e governador (UF). Se não gravou `fechou` nem `uf_fechou`, os fechamentos de UF saem do `-ab` br. Essas entradas têm id negativo e `derivado:true`.
  - **Banco ausente:** o servidor serve os estáticos; `/api/estado` responde `{pronto:false}` e `/api/config` vem com listas vazias. As outras rotas respondem 503, e o banco é reaberto quando aparecer.
  
  **SSE:** a sondagem roda a cada 500 ms. O `id:` é o id do snapshot e `Last-Event-ID` retoma do ponto; regressivos nunca são enviados. Há `retry: 2000`, recursos liberados quando o cliente cai e `idleTimeout` de 120 s.
  
  **Replay**
  - `bun run replay` leu o registro inteiro contra as fixtures em 3,7 s: 59.101 arquivos, 15 snapshots, 59.086 `nao_existe`, 0 `parse_error`.
  - `bun run replay --db x.sqlite --speed 60 --from ISO [--out data/replay.sqlite]`: copia o registro e a configuração, depois relê os blobs em ordem de `capturado_em`, mantendo as horas originais e pausando conforme o speed (`--speed 0` não pausa).
  - Usa um sequenciador próprio sobre as primitivas do B1 e não calcula anomalias. O `src/collector/processar.ts` do B2 acabou de chegar e pode entrar no lugar numa próxima passada.
  
  O `data/replay.sqlite` (ignorado pelo git) ficou do meu teste manual.

  **What each screen does**
  - **gov:** the map is coloured by the leader's campo, banded by pst. Full colour only when pst = 100 and the leader has more than 50% of valid votes; a tie shows neutral grey. Next to it is the 6-campo legend.
    - The panel is the 27-UF list (3×9). The lime "1º turno" mark appears only when the TSE resultado has `e` set or `st` starting with "eleit".
    - Key `m` uses `ui.variante % 2` to swap the map for "quem já decidiu": UFs with the count closed, with their TSE status.

  - **sen:** the 81-seat hemicycle.
    - 27 outlined seats are the 2022 senators.
    - The 54 filled seats are `top[0..1]` of mapa 6259/5. Leaders are banded by pst and capped below full colour; full colour only when the TSE marks the candidate elected. Seats with no data are grey.
    - Below it is the 40px composition bar with lines at 41, 49 and 54 and a legend.
    - The panel is the Senate-mode list: two campo dots and names per UF, pst in mono at the right.
    - Copy always says "líder" unless the TSE marks the candidate elected.
    - Needs are the same shape as gov, for cargo 5.
  - **proporcional:** sorts once per snapshot and draws only 18 rows. Each row has #, photo, name, TSE chip, party, votes, % of valid and a cumulative bar. The footer reads "outros N candidatos: X% dos nominais".
    - The panel has the campo bar (nominal votes in full colour, legenda votes lighter), the top 10 parties, and squares by campo.
    - Squares show elected per campo when the TSE marks `e`. Otherwise, at pst ≥ 70 they show the provisional allocation with a "projeção provisória" chip. Below 70% there is a sentence saying when it appears.
  - **ritmo:** a line chart of `historico` plus the current point since 17:00, with a dashed projection line to the 100% reference.
    - The hero number is sections per minute from a linear fit of the last 10 minutes (`ritmoRecente`); "previsão de 100%" is labelled as a naive projection.
    - The panel has the two latency histograms (with medians) and 27 UF bars, slowest first.
  - **mov:** lines for candidates who passed 10% of valid votes, labelled at the line ends. The y-axis is 30 to 60 when the data fits, otherwise fitted to the data. Lead changes from `viradas` get a circle and the time.
    - The panel has sparklines for SP, MG, RJ, BA, RS and PR (top two candidates, current margin, lead-change dots) and the latest leader changes from `anomalias`.
    - Needs: serie br, the 6 UF series, resultado br (names and colours), anomalias.
  - **Shared:** every empty state has a sentence (for example "nenhuma UF com seções totalizadas ainda"). Colours go through `style.fill` with transitions.
  
  **How `senadores_2022.json` is loaded and cached:** `carregarSenadores()` in `senado.ts` keeps one promise at module level. It runs `fetch('/senadores_2022.json')` once per page load and validates the rows with `lerSenadores` (campo goes through `campoValido`). Every later mount of `sen` reuses the same promise. A failure resets the promise so the next mount tries again. While the file is missing, the legend says the list has not loaded and those seats stay grey.
  
  **Seat allocation (`cadeiras.ts`, pure)**
  - **Electoral quotient:** valid votes divided by seats; a fraction of a half or less is dropped, above a half rounds up.
  - **Party quotient:** floor of the alliance's votes divided by the quotient, capped by its candidates with at least 10% of the quotient.
  - **Leftover seats:** highest averages among alliances with at least 80% of the quotient and unelected candidates with at least 20% of it.
  - **Third phase:** the remaining seats go to all alliances that still have a candidate, as the STF decided in 2024.
  - **Nobody reaches the quotient:** the most voted candidates win.
  - A federação counts as one alliance; candidates are grouped by `fed_sg || sg`.
  - **Tested cases:**
    - quotient rounding: 333, 167, 375, 500 and 0;
    - exact party quotient 5/3/2;
    - a leftover seat by highest average;
    - the 80% threshold, both sides of 1,600 votes;
    - the 10% cap, the 20% rule and the open third phase together;
    - no quotient reached, so the top candidates win;
    - zero votes, and the total never exceeding the seats.
  - In the mock only 60 of SP's 70 seats get allocated, because the mock has only 60 candidates and the rule has nobody left to seat.
  
  **Changes to F1 files (none made):**
  - `proporcional.ts` imports `tituloDaTela` from `views/index.ts`, which makes an import cycle through `registrar-legislativas`. It is harmless because the function is only called at runtime. Moving `tituloDaTela` and `TITULOS` into their own module would remove the cycle.
  - The `m` key already sets `ui.variante` globally and writes it to the hash, so `gov` reads it; no keyboard change was needed.
  
  **Deviations from the plan**
  - **Deputy rows:** 42px tall with a 40px photo instead of 40px rows with a 48px photo. 18 rows plus header and footer don't fit in the 832px stage otherwise.
  - **Hemicycle size:** the 640×330 reference is scaled 1.8×, not 2.2×. 2.2× would be about 1,408px wide and not fit in 1152; 1.8× gives exactly 1152×594.
  - **Long titles:** with three chips, "Deputados federais, São Paulo" is cut with an ellipsis by the existing title CSS.

  - **No zone or `zz` file returned 404.** All 30,717 zone files and every `zz` file exist. The 142 404s are only the expected ones:
    - 137 `-e` probe files;
    - 4 national `-u` files for election 6259 (probes);
    - `pe-c0025-e006261-u.json`: the UF-level file for Fernando de Noronha's municipal election does not exist. It is a T0 file, so it 404s every 30 s forever; harmless, but it could be dropped from the registry.
  - **"zz has 187 against 186" is not an unknown municipality.** The 187th entry in the `zz` `-ab` is the UF row (`tpabr` `uf`). No `municipio_desconhecido` fired.
  - **Schema drift found on live data:** candidates now carry a new field, `$.carg[].agr[].par[].cand[].subs` (probably the substitute). The zod `passthrough` keeps it in the blob, but nothing normalizes it.
  - `Accept-Encoding: gzip` set explicitly still gets decompressed by Bun.
  
  **Decisions where I departed from or filled in the plan**
  - **`-ab` fingerprints on restart** are rebuilt by re-parsing the latest stored `-ab` body for each file, not from `v_ab_atual`. The view stores `pst` as a number, so it cannot reproduce the original text and every municipality would look changed after a restart.
  - **The queue uses two heaps** (one waiting by due time, one ready by priority then due time), so a high-priority job due later never blocks an overdue sweep job. The ordering is still (priority, due).
  - **Capitals and cities with 200k+ voters get a minimum interval scaled from `APURACAO_MIN_INTERVALO_MU`**: 300 gives 120 s, 600 gives 240 s. This way the "slow down" knob also slows capitals.
  - **Schema drift detection** runs on at most 20 bodies per file type, level and cargo per run, because the strict check is expensive on 300 KB deputy files. Each drifted path is still reported once.
  - **Batch writes:** if a batch transaction fails, it is retried one job at a time, so one bad job does not lose the other 49. Database ids are never carried across batches.
  - **Sweep rate bucket:** only re-probes of T2/T3 files share the 40/s sweep bucket with sweep jobs; T4 probes do not.
  - **The first collector sweep starts right at startup**, and the first `-ab` snapshot does not fan out to municipalities.
  - **Restarts restart the sweep:** after each restart the sweep starts again from the beginning, skipping only files read in the last 300 s.
  - **Health JSON:** I added `heap_mb` to `meta.estado_coletor` next to `memoria_mb` (which is RSS).

    - `extrair-fotos.py` used to download the BR zip and then crash with a bare traceback when `consulta_cand_2026.zip` was missing. It now exits up front with a clear `SystemExit` naming the TSE URL and the expected path.
  - Dead script: `test:e2e` (`bunx playwright test`) has no `playwright.config.ts` and no spec files. I reported it and did not remove it.
  
  ### 2. Every view against `data/ensaio.sqlite` (new harness `qa/i2.ts`)
  Each row passed: scroll exactly the viewport size, nothing outside `#telao`, no page errors, zero requests off 127.0.0.1. 404s on `/api/resultado` and `/fotos` are expected in the rehearsal DB (only SP has governor and senate, no deputies, fictional senators) and are filtered out.

  Two telão defects found and fixed:
  - **Request leak.** `src/ui/data/api.ts` threw on a non-ok response without reading the body. Chromium then kept every 404 open. In `gov` replay, 26 pending requests piled up every 2 s, and the contrast audit could never reach networkidle. Over a 6-hour live night in OBS these would have kept accumulating. It now cancels the body before throwing (`r.body?.cancel()`), and the pending count stays at 0.
  - **Clipped row in fed-uf / est-uf.** The 18th row was cut by the footer at 1920×1080: 18 × 2.625rem is taller than the space available. `.pr-lista` is now a grid of 18 `minmax(0, 2.625rem)` rows. Checked with mock data in qa/i2-fed-uf-SP-mock.png.
  
  I re-ran the full I2 after these fixes: 21/21 OK.
  
  **Contrast** (`contrast-audit.py`, replay at 23:00, hud=0): `gov`, `sen`, `fed-uf&uf=SP`, `ritmo`, `mov` all **0 below WCAG AA**. Extra runs, also 0:
  - fed-uf on `#mock=1`, because the rehearsal DB has no deputy data.
  - espera on the real baseline.
  
  ### 3. Server on the real baseline (port 4190)
  **Bug found and fixed first.** The server answered 503 "banco ainda não existe" on every data endpoint. The DB is in WAL mode and had no `-shm` (the clean checkpoint and preflight's sqlite3 had removed it). A `readonly` connection cannot create the `-shm`, so SQLite refuses to open the file. This would happen any time the server starts while the collector is stopped, for example a restart after the night or a replay of the real DB.
  - Fix in `abrirLeitura`: try readonly first; if that fails, the file exists and there is no `-shm`, open with `readwrite, create:false`. `query_only=1` still blocks writes.
  - New test in `tests/schema.test.ts`: red without the fix, green with it. It also checks that the reader sees a later write and that a missing file is still neither opened nor created.
  
  Timings (curl total, first call | cached):
  
  | endpoint | before | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | /api/config (490 KB) | 53 ms \| 2 ms | 62 ms \| 2 ms |
  | /api/estado | 2 ms | 2 ms |
  | mapa 6257/1 uf | 2 ms | 2 ms |
  | mapa 6257/1 mun sp | 12 ms | 9 ms |
  | **mapa 6259/7 mun sp** | **390 ms** \| 0.7 ms | **44 ms** \| 0.9 ms |
  | resultado 6259/7 sp71072 (255 KB) | 3 ms | 3 ms |
  | serie 6257/1 br | 1 ms | 1 ms |
  | anomalias | 8 ms | 8 ms |
  | fim 6257/1 | 10 ms | 9 ms |
  
  - **What made the deputies map slow.** `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` was already fully indexed. The cost was the `ROW_NUMBER()` window sorting about 1.16M rows (645 municipalities × about 1,800 candidates each).
  - **Fix: a query change, no index.** I chose not to add an index because it would add write cost on `voto_candidato`, the biggest table on the night. `candidatosMapa` now cuts to the top N per snapshot first (an `IN (SELECT ... ORDER BY vap DESC, sqcand LIMIT ?)` subquery) and then ranks. Output is byte-identical on SP and MG. The other heavy maps after the fix: MG deputados estaduais 47 ms, SP deputados federais 37 ms.
  - The telão only requests majoritarian maps, but the cache invalidates on every new snapshot, so this matters for any live caller.
  
  **Telão against port 4190:** shows `espera`, and after 12 s at dwell=3 it is still on espera, so rotation stays paused.
  - eleitorado shows **157,83 mi** (27 UFs, real `-ab`); seções **499.248** (equals the sum of per-UF `ts`).
  - municípios showed **5.757** because it counted the exterior cities. I fixed `espera.ts` to exclude ZZ, so it now shows **5.571**.
  - Still mixed: seções (499.248) is the TSE national total including abroad, while eleitorado excludes it. I left that for you to decide. Screenshot: qa/i2-real-espera.png.
  
  ### 4. Registry
  - Added `existeNoTse(k)` in `src/tse/urls.ts`, which returns false for `u` at `nivel=uf` with `ele=6261`. `registroDeArquivos` skips that key.
  - The live DB already has the `u:6261:25:uf:pe::` row, so `EstadoArquivos.carregarDoBanco` now also skips it. The row stays in the DB for audit but is never enqueued; without this the existing DB would keep polling it.
  - Kept for 6261: `e:6261:25:uf:pe::` (one of the 137 `-e` probes), `mu`, `zona`, `ab br` and `ab pe`. The live DB shows both `-ab` files as `nao_modificado`, so they exist.
  - Tests: `tests/urls.test.ts` now asserts no 6261 `u uf`, keeps the `e` probe and both `-ab`, and the total `u uf` count is 28 + 27×4. `tests/estado-arquivos.test.ts` asserts a legacy row is excluded from the loaded state.

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below).

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Offer cloud first

Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true:
- the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or
- regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday")

If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first:
- `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?"
- `header`: "Schedule"
- `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]`

If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now").
If they pick **This session only**:
- If the trigger was a parsed ≥60-minute interval (rule 1 or 2): continue below with that interval.
- If the trigger was daily phrasing only (rule 3, no parsed interval): do NOT call CronCreate. Explain that a daily-cadence loop won't fire before this session closes, so there's nothing useful to schedule locally — suggest they either pick Cloud schedule, or re-run `/loop` with an explicit shorter interval (e.g. `/loop 1h <prompt>`) if they want a session loop. Then stop.
If neither trigger condition was met: continue below.

## Fixed-interval mode (rules 1 and 2)

Convert the interval to a cron expression:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

Then:
1. Call CronCreate with: `cron` (the expression above), `prompt` (the parsed prompt verbatim), `recurring: true`.
2. Briefly confirm: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days, and that the user can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID). Only if you did NOT show the cloud-offer AskUserQuestion above (i.e., neither trigger condition applied), end the confirmation with this exact line on its own, italicized: `_Runs until you close this session · For durable cloud-based loops, use /schedule_`. If the user already answered that question, omit this line.
3. **Then immediately execute the parsed prompt now** — don't wait for the first cron fire. If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.

## Dynamic mode (rule 3 — no interval)

The user wants you to self-pace. Decide what makes the next iteration worth running — a passage of time, or an observable event.

1. **Run the parsed prompt now.** If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.
2. **If the next run is gated on an event** (CI finishing, a log line matching, a file changing, a PR comment) and no Monitor is already running for it: arm one now with `timeout_ms: 1800000`. Its events arrive as `<task-notification>` messages and wake this loop immediately — you do not wait for the ScheduleWakeup deadline. A monitor expires after at most 30 minutes and tells you; on later iterations call TaskList first and re-arm only if no monitor for it is still running.
3. **Briefly confirm**: that you're self-pacing, whether a Monitor is the primary wake signal, that you ran the task now, and what fallback delay you're about to pick. This must be ordinary visible response text — the user cannot see your thinking/reasoning, so an update written only there is invisible to them. Write it immediately BEFORE calling ScheduleWakeup — on this model the turn ends as soon as that tool returns, so an update after the call never goes out.
4. **Then, as the last action of this turn, decide whether the loop continues.** If the task needs another iteration, call ScheduleWakeup with:
   - `delaySeconds`: with a Monitor armed this is the **fallback heartbeat** — how long to wait if no event fires (lean 1200–1800s; idle ticks more frequent than the task needs are pure overhead). Without a Monitor this is the cadence — pick based on what you observed. Read the tool's own description for cache-aware delay guidance.
   - `reason`: one short sentence on why you picked that delay.
   - `prompt`: the full original /loop input verbatim, prefixed with `/loop ` so the next firing re-enters this skill and continues the loop. For example, if the user typed `/loop check the deploy`, pass `/loop check the deploy` as the prompt.
   - `noop`: `true` if this tick changed nothing ("still waiting", "quiet hold"); `false` if it did something worth keeping. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks collapse in the terminal.
   If it doesn't need another iteration, stop instead (step 6) — re-arming is a per-turn choice, not a default.
5. **If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`** rather than this prompt: handle the event in the context of the loop task, then make the same decision. If the loop should continue, write the same brief update as visible text, then call ScheduleWakeup again with the same `prompt` and the same 1200–1800s `delaySeconds` from the schedule step above (the Monitor remains the wake signal; the new wakeup is only the fallback heartbeat). If the event means the work is finished, stop (step 6).
6. **To stop the loop** — the task is complete, further iterations can't make progress, or the user asked you to stop — call ScheduleWakeup with `stop: true` (no other fields) and TaskStop any Monitor you armed (use TaskList to find the task ID if it is no longer in context). Then write the loop's outcome for the user as ordinary visible response text — a stopped loop has no next tick to surface it. Stopping is the loop's normal ending — the user can restart it anytime with /loop. Before you stop, send a one-line outcome via PushNotification — the user may be away and waiting to hear it's done. Skip this if you're stopping because the user just told you to; they're already here.

  - By bloc: direita + centro-direita 291, esquerda + centro-esquerda 137, centro 85.
  - Largest parties: PL 121, PT 70, UNIÃO 46, PSD 44, PP 41, REPUBLICANOS 40, MDB 36.
  
  **Senado 2027 (81 seats = 27 elected in 2022 + 54 now).** 13 UFs are TSE-final and 14 provisional.
  - By campo: esquerda 16, centro-esquerda 1, centro 12, centro-direita 17, direita 35.
  - By bloc: direita + centro-direita 52, esquerda + centro-esquerda 17, centro 12.
  - By party: PL 27, PT 10.
  - **No 2023 comparison:** `analysis/senado_2026/` only has the 27 senators elected in 2022, not the full 2023 Senate, so the comparison is skipped and the output says so.
  - **Cleitinho:** he is "indefinido" in `public/senadores_2022.json`, so I applied the rule from `analysis/senado_2026/metodo.md` and count him as direita and Republicanos.
  
  **Assemblies (both provisional)**
  - **SP (94 seats):** esquerda 28, centro-esquerda 1, centro 14, centro-direita 16, direita 35 (blocs 51 / 29 / 14). PL 27, PT 16. Most voted: Eduarda Campopiano (PL) 1.956.143.
  - **MG (77 seats):** esquerda 22, centro-esquerda 3, centro 11, centro-direita 13, direita 28 (blocs 41 / 25 / 11). PL 21, PT 15. Most voted: Bruno Engler (PL) 675.675.
  
  **Governors: 20 elected in the 1st round, 7 states going to a 2nd round.**
  - Of the 20 elected in the 1st round, 8 are direita, 4 centro-direita, 5 centro and 3 esquerda.
  - 2nd round in AC, AM, DF, ES, RJ, RN, TO.
  - **Cutoff races:** Moro wins PR outright with 50.10% at 99.99% of sections, which is still provisional. Celina Leão (DF) missed by 49.93% and Mailza (AC) by 49.76%.
  - **Gov vs president:** the governor's campo differs from the campo of the presidential leader in that UF in 14 UFs (10 by bloc).
  - **Vão estadual:** PB +31.2, AL +11.7, SP Tarcísio × Flávio +10.7, MG Cleitinho × Flávio +7.1.
  
  **Odd things in the data**
  1. **TSE marks 2nd-round candidates as elected.** In the final files, `eleito = 1` is set for "2º turno" candidates too. On my first run that made AC, DF, ES, RJ and TO look decided. The script now decides by the `st` text (it must start with "Eleito").
  2. **Possible bug in the dashboard seat projection (I did not touch it).** `src/ui/views/proporcional.ts:55` adds `tvtn + tvan` per party. In the DB, `tvan` is nominal votes plus votes annulled sub judice, so nominal votes are counted twice and legenda votes are left out. It should be `tvtn + tvtl`; with `tvan`, MDB in Acre would get 2 seats instead of 1. This affects the dashboard's provisional seat squares.
  3. **The national file stall was longer than the stated one.** The national presidential file stopped at 18:48:59. It produced only one new version, at 19:14:08, and was then stuck again until 20:04:39 (51 more minutes): 97 fetches, none with a change. So in 76 minutes it moved 47.3% → 64.8% → 85.0%. The UF files stall matches what you gave: 19:32:28 to 20:02:02.
  4. **100% without TSE final status.** RJ shows 100% of sections but no final flag from the TSE for federal deputy, and so do RJ and RS for state deputy, so they stay provisional.
  5. **Only 13 UFs hit 100% for president:** DF first at 20:12, RJ last at 22:07.
  6. **Senate second seats are very close:** PA Chicão × Éder Mauro differ by 0.16 points (14.393 votes) and RN Samanda × Zenaide by 0.16 points (5.741 votes), both provisional.
  7. **Other facts:**
     - First national totalization at 17:21:16.
     - Exterior: Lula 47.50% × Flávio 43.54%.
     - Turnout 78.83%; blank 1.84% and null 2.93% of votes.
     - The DB has 469.769 snapshots and 2.178.394 fetches, 8,47 GB.
     - Deltan Dallagnol (PR) comes out elected; his votes are still marked valid.
     - Marina JHC and JHC count as centro-direita through the per-candidate exceptions in `campos.json`.

O que o TSE disse sobre a parada, por Nunes Marques e em quase todas as matérias:
- Houve "congestionamento de dados" no sistema de divulgação, com fluxo acima do normal. A TI isolou outros sistemas e depois religou, com protocolo de segurança. Ele disse que a totalização não foi afetada e que o TSE "tinha absoluto controle da situação".
- Ele descartou interferência externa e não houve menção a ataque em nenhuma matéria lida.
- Não há explicação para a causa do fluxo, para o fato de só o arquivo presidencial ter parado, nem para o salto de 64,81% para 84,96% de uma vez. Também não aparece nenhum relatório técnico prometido.

**Horários.** A imprensa data a parada pelo painel, de 19h06 a 20h08, com 64,81% subindo a 84,96%. As marcas do nosso coletor são outras (18:48:59 a 19:14:08, depois até 20:04:39, e 47,26% no app às 19:08). São camadas diferentes e não se contradizem, mas o dossiê deve citar cada uma com sua fonte. A duração também varia entre veículos, de 40 minutos a uma hora. As urnas trocadas aparecem como 1.121, 1.543 e 1.861 conforme a hora do balanço.

**Alegações de fraude.** Não achei nenhuma campanha ou parlamentar acusando fraude na apuração ou na parada do painel naquela noite. O que apareceu:
- Eduardo Bolsonaro disse que o governo prepara narrativa de anulação por interferência dos EUA (Tribuna do Sertão, sem hora).
- A PF abriu procedimento preliminar sobre suposta interferência dos EUA (CNN Brasil).
- Circulou publicação com Flávio vencedor em mais de uma dúzia de países com urnas ainda abertas (ICL Notícias, autores não nomeados).
- Descartei por serem de 2022 o item do Aos Fatos e o da CNN sobre Valdemar.

**Incidentes e exterior.** Quatro mortes por mal súbito, dois candidatos presos, boca de urna e compra de votos no topo das ocorrências, e temporal no Paraná sem interromper a votação. A busca por facção, milícia e coação não devolveu matéria do dia, e isso não prova ausência. No exterior, Moscou votou em papel porque a urna "ficou presa em Madri", e houve filas em Londres, Paris e Milão.

- tse.jus.br devolveu 429 (WebFetch e curl), então a nota oficial do TSE não foi lida direto. Registrei o item como existência confirmada pelo buscador, com o conteúdo conferido pela cópia do Senado e por agências.

**Top 8 polls by |error on L−F|** (valid votes; error = poll minus urn; ● = institute's last wave):

- **Common error repeated 2022.** Across the last wave of 13 institutes the error on L−F was +3.55 (median +4.04, spread between houses 3.15); in 2022 it was +3.92. 10 of 13 overestimated Lula. 7 of 13 missed by more than their own sampling margin.
- **Proximity is not accuracy.** The two closest institutes (Futura, Gerp) were, before the election, the most pro-Flávio relative to peers. Futura is the only house within its sampling margin in both 2022 and 2026, and both times it erred toward the right. With errors spread as observed, there was about a 60% chance that some house would land within 0.5 point by dispersion alone.
- **Income reweighting.** It cut the aggregator's L−F error from +4.25 to +2.43. Of the 10 reweightable last waves, 7 moved toward the urn; Quaest moved away and Gerp overshot.

**Arvor's central forecast:** Flávio 45.26 (−1.77), Lula 45.12 (−0.05), third parties 9.63 (+1.82); error on L−F +1.72. Only 4 of 13 final polls did better.
- The urn's margin fell at the 77th percentile of the Monte Carlo distribution. The forecast gave Flávio a 52% chance of finishing ahead, so it called a tie, not the winner.
- The urn's third-party share (7.81) was below the forecast's 5th percentile (8.67).
- The miss is in the Centro-Sul (L−F error +3.81); the Nordeste reverses sign (−1.72) and the Norte is −0.23.
- The UF leader was right in 25 of 27 UFs (missed AM and AP). The DLM anchor erred +2.27.

**Senate:** Brier score 0.0569 over 303 candidacies, against 0.093 for "top two in the poll average, with certainty" and 0.146 for equal probabilities. The two most likely candidates per UF won 42 of 54 seats, against 36.5 expected from the forecast's own probabilities. **Governors:** the predicted leader led in 24 of 27 UFs; 20 races decided in the first round against 17.6 expected; Brier on "decided in the first round" 0.127.

**Voto útil decomposition** (estimate, not a measurement):
- Third parties fell from 12.14 in the final polls to 7.81 at the urn. Renan lost 1.38, Caiado 1.20, Cury 0.69, Zema 0.63; Flávio gained 3.94 over the polls and Lula 0.39.
- The urn revealed a median 61% of Flávio's second-round reserve, against 12% of Lula's.
- Of the +3.55 error on L−F, consolidation explains +1.46 (41%) using the Nexus transfer matrix renormalized between Flávio and Lula. Across the variants the range is +0.94 to +1.83 (27% to 52%). The remaining +1.72 to +2.60 is a shift between the two finalists that consolidation does not explain; the data cannot separate house effect, late swing and differential turnout.
- Assumptions are in the memo: the second-round matrix applied to the first round, a matrix from one institute measured 18–20/09, minor candidates split 50/50, abstention not modeled.

<result>All 35 catalog figures are in place and pass every check. The interactivity is wired in: I added a single line in `pagina_view.py`, so the writer doesn't need to do anything. Nothing is committed.

**Checks:** the build runs with all 15 chapters complete and zero "—". Contrast audit: 0 below WCAG AA. Render audit: 0 problems. `ruff` and `black` are clean, and `pytest tests/test_apuracao_2026_*.py` passes (222 tests). The Playwright QA hovers the first, middle and last target of all 35 figures: the card always appears and never leaves the figure box. It also checks that a tap opens the card and a tap elsewhere hides it, that nothing throws console errors, and that the page doesn't scroll sideways at 390 px.

**Implemented names** (all registered in `FIGURAS`, and the writer's `fig("nome", d)` calls already resolve):
- **Cap. 1–3, 14:** `placar_candidatos`, `acumulado_noite`, `lotes_noite`, `divergencia_nacional`, `latencia_hora`, `auditoria_coletor`
- **Maps:** `mapa_vencedor_uf`, `mapa_hora_100`, `governadores_mapa`, `mapa_swing_uf`, `mapa_mundi_exterior`, `mapa_anomalias`
- **Regions and exterior:** `regioes_2022_2026`, `dispersao_municipios`, `capitais_interior`, `comparecimento_regioes`, `exterior_continentes`
- **Congress:** `hemiciclo_camara`, `camara_partidos`, `votos_x_cadeiras`, `camara_por_uf`, `hemiciclo_senado`, `senado_segundas_vagas`, `assembleias_campo`, `vao_estadual`
- **Polls and voto útil:** `pesquisas_erro`, `pesquisas_serie`, `central_casa_ufs`, `senado_brier`, `voto_util_cascata`, `reserva_vs_urna`
- **Anomalies and 2nd round:** `anomalias_features`, `transferencia_cenarios`, `estoque_uf`, `movimentos_2t`

**Interactivity hook:** `interativo_html()` in `pagina_interativo.py` emits one `&lt;style&gt;` and one `&lt;script&gt;`. It's added in `pagina_view.py` right after the existing `&lt;script&gt;{JS}&lt;/script&gt;`. Without JS, each figure still renders whole in its default view.

**Things that behave differently from what you might assume:**
- **Module split:** the old primitives moved to `pagina_figuras_prim.py`. `pagina_figuras.py` now holds the registry and re-exports every old function, so existing calls keep working.
- **Bad data:** a figure whose data is missing returns a `&lt;figure class="pendente"&gt;` and prints a warning. It never raises, so one bad dataset can't take down a chapter.
- **Darker campo shades:** inside figures, centro is `#7d6e33` and centro-direita `#4373b8` instead of the house `#8a7a3a` / `#4f7fc2`. The house colours fail 4.5:1 under labels with both white and ink text. Tables and other HTML outside the figures still use the house values, so the two can differ slightly.
- **Map legends are HTML** under the map, not inside the SVG, so they stay readable at 390 px. Maps never scroll; the hemicycle now scrolls internally below 720 px.
- **Page size:** the page is now 2.64 MB. Most of that is the dispersion chart with all 5,570 municipalities (about 550 KB) and the four municipal maps (about 470 KB, roughly 112 KB gzipped).

**Data fields I needed and didn't find:**
- **Senate:** `senado.json` has Cleitinho with `campo: "indefinido"`. I used `comparacao_2022.senado.classe_2022`, which has the declared exception (direita); with it the Senate count matches 52.
- **`auditoria_coletor`:** there are no read classes per hour (ok, 304, timeout and so on); those exist only for the 29-minute gap. I used versions read per hour plus the regressive-flag classes per hour, and put the gap's read classes in the caption.
- **`pesquisas_serie`:** the aggregator series is in % of all respondents, so I renormalised it to valid votes to compare with the urn. It has gaps on days with no poll in the window, and I left those as gaps rather than joining them.

<summary>Agent "W5: coleta e decodificação dos boletins de urna por seção" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I've built the section downloader and BU decoder, Acre passed the zone-by-zone check exactly, and the national run is going in the background (pid 27950, `data/secoes.pid`). Nothing is committed. `bun run check` is green (339 tests) and ruff/black are clean on the one Python file.

**The voting machine model is not in the BU.** The TSE BU format for 2026 (`bu.asn1` v2, the 2024 format) has no `modeloUrna` field, so the UE2009=9 … UE2020=20 enum you expected doesn't exist. The model comes from the urna's own log (`-log.jez`), which has a line like `Identificação do Modelo de Urna: UE2020` (text, not a code). The log's third column is the urna's internal number, the same value the BU records as `numeroInternoUrna`. I match on that, so the model belongs to the urna that wrote the BU even if the log lists several urnas.

**Where the fields live in the BU:**
- **Section:** municipality, zone, polling place and section number.
- **`urna` block:**
  - `tipoUrna`: 1 secao, 3 contingencia, 4 reservaSecao, 6 reservaEncerrandoSecao.
  - `versaoVotacao`, e.g. "10.23.0.0 - Praia da Barra do Cahy".
  - `numeroInternoUrna`: the urna's internal number, stored as `numero_interno_urna`.
  - `tipoArquivo`: 1 is a normal urna file, 2 a recovered one, 3 to 6 are Sistema de Apuração.
- **`dadosSecao`:** first vote, last vote and BU issue time, in the urna's local time.
- **Results per election (federal 6257, state 6259):**
  - Eligible voters, split into the section's own voters and in-transit (TTE) voters. The federal and state counts differ by the TTE voters.
  - Turnout per office.
  - Vote type per entry: 1 nominal, 2 branco, 3 nulo, 4 legenda.

I checked my TypeScript decoder field by field against an independent decode (`asn1tools`) of the SP 71072/0001/0001 sample.

**Acre acceptance result:** 115 of 115 checks match exactly (23 municipality-zone pairs × 5 offices). For president, the sum of 2,270 BUs equals the zone files:

| | Sections sum = zone files |
|---|---|
| Eligible voters | 613,742 |
| Turnout | 488,330 |
| Brancos | 5,579 |
| Nulos | 13,662 |
| Flávio | 302,807 |
| Lula | 134,770 |

Every candidate matches, and the totals also equal the Acre statewide file. The 23 "technical null" votes are votes for number 28, which is on the urna but not in the result list. Acre has 141 aggregated sections; they have no BU of their own (their voters are in the main section's BU).

**I corrected the check twice after it flagged mismatches**, and both were rule errors on my side, not data problems:
- **Rondônia, federal deputy:** a party whose list is "Anulado sub judice" (MOBILIZA, 33) shows 0 valid party votes, but its counted party votes are in a separate field. I now compare against the counted figure.
- **Sergipe, federal deputy:** votes for a party with no list for that office are counted as technical nulls. In zone 15 of municipality 31003, the 5 technical nulls are exactly 1 vote for an unlisted candidate plus 2 + 2 party votes for parties 25 and 77.

Both cases now have regression tests. After re-checking, the eight finished areas (AC, AP, RR, RO, TO, SE, DF and the exterior sample) pass 1,779 of 1,779 checks, including the DF district deputy office.

**Throughput and ETA:**
- About 25 sections/s at 73 requests/s. Each section needs three requests: aux file, BU and log.
- The default rate is 74/s, so with the collector's ~6/s the total stays at or under 80.
- Zero 403/429 and zero failed sections so far.
- 488,824 sections remained at 09:11 UTC, so it should finish around 14:30 UTC (11:30 BRT).
- The full log has to be downloaded to read the model (the server ignores partial requests), so expect about 45 GB of transfer. The database should end near 9 GB.

**Database:** everything is in `data/secoes_2026.sqlite`; `uf` is lowercase and `mun` is the 5-digit TSE code, matching `apuracao.sqlite`.
- **`secao`**: one row per section listed in the TSE section-config file, with the raw aux file, chosen hash, receipt time, the BU gzipped, log size and SHA-256, and any error.
- **`bu`**: decoded fields per section, including model, how the model was found, urna number, urna and file type, eligible voters (with in-transit), turnout, biometrics, first and last vote, load data and the urnas found in the log.
- **`bu_cargo`** (added): eligible voters and turnout per office. I added it because the federal and state voter counts differ, so the president's denominator must come from the federal election.
- **`voto_secao`**: votes per section, office, vote type and number (0 for branco and nulo).
- **`conferencia_zona`**: the zone-by-zone check, with each mismatch listed.
- **`v_voto_secao`**: a view joining votes with the model and the per-office denominators.

**Other choices you should know about:**
- **Paper-ballot sections:** some sections, mostly abroad, have a Sistema de Apuração BU (`-busa.dat`) instead of a normal one. I fetch it, and it is preferred when both exist. These have no urna log, so their model is empty.
- **Logs not kept:** only each log's size, SHA-256 and the model and urna numbers are stored. `--guardar-log` keeps the raw file.
- **`imgbu`:** not fetched.

**Running it:**
- Progress goes to `data/logs/secoes-20261005.log` every 15 s.
- `kill -TERM $(cat data/secoes.pid)` stops it cleanly, and rerunning the script resumes where it left off.
- At the end, run `--refazer-erros` (retry failed sections) and then `--so-conferir` (re-check all complete zones).

The collector and its code were not modified; the new scripts only import its rate limiter and JSON parsers.

Files are in `/Users/leonardodias/arvor/PNAD/apuracao` (`package.json` also gained a `secoes` script):
- `scripts/secoes-coletar.ts`
- `scripts/bu-decode.ts`
- `scripts/bu-referencia.py`
- `scripts/secoes/` (`banco.ts`, `conferencia.ts`, `cs.ts`, `http.ts`, `log-urna.ts`, `secao.ts`, `bu.asn1`)
- `tests/bu-decode.test.ts`
- `tests/secoes.test.ts`
- `tests/fixtures/bu/`

**Checks on the real partial JSON**

**Two things you need to know before the final build**
- **The committed `docs/apuracao_1o_turno_2026.html` does not have the section part yet.** W6's JSON was still uncommitted and changing while I worked, so I reverted the page to the committed version rather than publish numbers from it. Run `python3 scripts/apuracao-2026-build.py` once the collection closes.
- **I ran `black` on all of `scripts/` once, while W6's `secoes_*.py` files were still untracked.** If they weren't already formatted, black may have reformatted them. W6 should check their diff. After that I only ran black on my own files.

**What the text says with the partial data**
- Lula has 90% or more in 313 sections and Flávio in 31.
- **Against the rest of the zone these sections look extreme:** a median of +40.5 points over the zone for Lula.
- **Against the same section in 2022 they don't:** 160 of the 163 matched Lula sections already gave him 80% or more. The text publishes this as the finding against the hasty reading.
- **Model of voting machine:** inside the same zone there is no effect separable from zero (Flávio −0.38 point, interval −0.95 to +0.20). Two pairs with only 4 to 8 zones do exclude zero; the text names them and says the interval is fragile with so few zones. The hypothesis box names the TRE machine allocation plan as the document that settles it.
- **Clusters:** the four groups are not geography (Cramér's V about 0.13 to 0.16). They separate sections by their pattern of zero votes, using W6's interpretation sentence. The text says k = 4 was your choice and that BIC prefers k = 5.

**Figures** (code in `pagina_fig_secoes.py` and `pagina_fig_secoes_b.py`; catalog has 43 entries; each has a hover card and keeps working in `file://`)
1. `secoes_90`: mirrored histogram (square-root scale, 90%+ band shaded, toggle for sections with 100+ voters) beside a map of the 222 polling places. Points are grouped into 0.25° cells if they ever pass 6,000.
2. `secoes_excesso`: 90%+ sections by how the rest of the zone votes, plus those 10 and 20 points above it.
3. `secoes_tamanho_tipo`: share of 90%+ sections by number of voters, type of place and machine model, with the national rate as a dashed line.
4. `clusters_secoes`: PCA scatter of about 8,000 sections, the 200 least likely outlined, a gold cloud on the most atypical group, a toggle to colour by region, a side panel for the four groups, and the table of 20 samples.
5. `clusters_regiao`: regional make-up of each group.
6. `modelo_urna_uf`: machine models per UF, grouped by region, in one colour from light to dark.
7. `modelo_urna_zona`: difference between models with 95% intervals, the raw difference as a hollow diamond, and three toggles (same zone, same building, 2022 same zone). The Registro table sits below.
8. `secoes_outras`: six panels (arrivals by hour, closing time, file type, machine type, loads, rare conditions). An empty panel says "nenhuma seção nesta condição".

**Text** is in the new `pagina_texto_c.py` and goes after the zone part of `r_anomalias`. It has six subheadings, from "Da zona para a seção" to "O que a seção prova e o que não prova", figure before paragraph, every number from the JSON, labelled by type, with the household analogy box and the hypothesis box. Elsewhere:
- `pagina_view.py` lists the new JSON and script for reproduction.
- `pagina_css.py` has the new styles.
- `pagina_interativo.py` gained a stroke-colour toggle (`data-as`).
- The fixture is `tests/fixtures/apuracao_2026/secoes_fixture.json`.
- New tests cover each figure, partial and complete coverage, empty lists, map grouping above 6,000 points, and the new subheadings in chapter 12.

**Where the real JSON differs from the contract** (I adapted to the JSON and didn't edit either):
- `excesso.*.faixas_zona` has 6 bands with different labels, not the 4 in the contract. The text uses "zone already at 80% or more", summing the bands.
- `outras.horarios.histograma_encerramento[].hora` is a "AAAA-MM-DD HH" string, not an integer.
- `urna.dentro_zona.pares` includes an extra summary pair, "mais nova" against "mais velha" (74 zones).
- `extremos.comparacao_2022` is available, with matched sections, although the contract expects it unavailable. The text uses it.
- `urna.ano_2022` (the same estimator on 2022, with `bolsonaro_pp` instead of `flavio_pp`) is new. It became the third toggle, showing the 6 pairs with most zones out of 16.
- `urna.registro`: `disponivel_2026` is false (SP not collected yet), there is no `leitura`, `dentro_zona_2026` is null, and 2022 uses `bolsonaro_pct`.
- `extremos.secoes_100pct` has `lula_total` and `flavio_total`, which I use for the count.
- `extremos.mapa.pontos` has no UF, so the UF on each map card comes from point-in-polygon on the IBGE outline. The `secoes` column, which sets the dot size, is the polling place's total number of sections.
- Extra keys I don't use yet: `clusters.variantes`, `sensibilidade`, `cramer_v_*`, `outras.amostras_nao_padrao`, `urna.dentro_zona_variacao`, `cobertura.secoes_com_erro`.
- The word "fraude" appears in W6's Benford warning, in a negative sense. I loosened my test so that sentence is allowed.

**Page size:** the page grows about 760 KB with the real data, to 3.4 MB. Most of it is the PCA scatter (about 366 KB for 8,000 points with hover cards).

<summary>Agent "W6: análise por seção (90%, GMM k=4, modelo de urna)" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>O pipeline das quatro perguntas (A a D) está pronto, commitado e no `origin/main`, rodando em modo `--parcial`. O lint e o black passam em `scripts` e `tests`, e os 31 testes passam. Não toquei no coletor. A base parcial tem 76.315 seções válidas, 15% das 499.248 do país; as UFs completas são AC, AL, AP, DF, ES, MS, MT, RN, RO, RR, SE e TO. Tudo abaixo muda quando entrarem SP, MG, BA, Nordeste e exterior.

**A. Seções com 90% ou mais.** Lula tem 321 seções nessa faixa (296 com 100 votantes ou mais, 79.123 aptos). Flávio tem 31. Com 100% dos válidos, são 10 de Lula e nenhuma de Flávio.
- **Achado contrário à tese:** 164 dessas seções de Lula existem em 2022 com o mesmo número e o mesmo local. Delas, 128 já davam 90% ou mais a Lula no 1º turno de 2022, 161 davam 80% ou mais, e a variação mediana foi de 0,8 ponto.
- **Onde estão:** 222 das 321 ficam em locais cujo nome indica aldeia ou escola indígena (inferência por palavra-chave). Nesta base parcial elas são enclaves: o resto da zona dá a Lula 54,5% (mediana).
- **Comparecimento e votos zerados:** nenhuma seção tem comparecimento acima de 100% e só uma tem 100% (52 aptos). Entre as seções com 200 votantes ou mais, nenhuma tem zero voto em Lula. Uma tem zero voto em Flávio: Confresa (MT), zona 28, seção 200, Escola Estadual Tapi'itawa, uma escola indígena.

**B. Mistura gaussiana com k = 4.** Rodei como foi pedido: frações sobre os aptos, zero trocado por 0,0001, log-razão centrada e ajuste em 14 coordenadas para a covariância não ficar singular.
- **Achado contrário:** os quatro grupos não são geografia (V de Cramér entre grupo e região 0,09). Eles separam as seções pelo padrão de zeros: 44% das células são zero, e a mistura se apoia nesse degrau. A especificação também é instável: o índice de Rand ajustado contra outra semente fica entre 0,02 e 0,18.
- **Versão de cinco partes** (Lula, Flávio, terceiros, brancos e nulos somados, abstenção): aqui os grupos seguem a geografia (V = 0,42). Saem um grupo nordestino de Lula, um de Flávio no Centro-Oeste e Sudeste, um de Flávio do agro (MT, MS, RO) e um pequeno grupo disperso. O grupo mais atípico é formado por aldeias, seções minúsculas e unidades prisionais.

**C. Modelo de urna.**
- **2026:** dentro da mesma zona, a urna mais nova dá a Flávio 0,00 ponto em relação à mais velha (IC 95% de −0,70 a 0,69, 79 zonas), contra −1,34 na comparação bruta. No mesmo prédio, +0,13 (IC 95% de −0,01 a 0,28, 2.318 locais).
- **2022:** dentro da zona, a UE2020 dá a Bolsonaro +1,41 ponto em relação à UE2015 (IC 95% de 0,84 a 2,00, não contém o zero). No mesmo prédio, +0,85 (IC 95% de −0,15 a 2,04, contém o zero, só 85 locais). A explicação provável é a alocação das urnas dentro da zona; o que resolve é o plano de alocação do TRE.
- **Teste novo de troca de urna:** comparei as mesmas seções que tinham urna velha em 2022 e nova em 2026. A urna mais nova dá a Flávio, sobre Bolsonaro, −0,18 ponto (IC 95% de −0,85 a 0,49), mas só 22 zonas entram por enquanto. Com SP e MG esse fica o teste mais forte.
- **Registro (SP) em 2022:** zona única, 154 seções. A UE2020 deu 58,9% a Bolsonaro e a UE2010 deu 52,5%. O controle por prédio não tem força em Registro, porque quase nenhum local mistura os dois modelos. O teste decisivo é ver como as mesmas seções votaram em 2026, e ele roda sozinho quando SP for coletado.

**D. Outras checagens.**
- **Arquivos não padrão:** tipo de arquivo recuperado e urnas de reserva não votam diferente da própria zona (Flávio −0,06 ponto nas urnas de reserva).
- **Encerramento tardio:** 1.194 seções fecharam depois das 19h e dão a Lula 2,72 pontos acima do resto da zona. A hipótese de fila em seção grande ainda precisa ser conferida com a ata.
- **Boletins depois de meia-noite:** zero por enquanto, porque MA, MG, BA e AM ainda não foram coletados. Cajari e os demais casos do BRIEF aparecem quando essas UFs entrarem.
- **Benford:** o teste rejeita a distribuição esperada, como se espera numa amostra deste tamanho. O texto registra que é só curiosidade metodológica e cita Deckert, Myagkov e Ordeshook (2011).

**Ficou de fora ou pendente:**
- **Zonas divergentes:** 1.143 seções saíram como zona divergente. Conferi que as 5 zonas sem arquivo congelado do TSE têm seções com erro de rede. Elas voltam depois de `--refazer-erros` e `--so-conferir` no coletor.
- **Zonas congeladas:** outras 158 seções estão em zonas cujo arquivo do TSE congelou incompleto.
- **Exterior:** ainda pendente na coleta.
- **Votos de 2022 por seção:** o CDN do TSE respondeu, e baixei `votacao_secao_2022_BR.zip` (271 MB). O total nacional de 2022 bate com o oficial.

**O que mudei além do pedido:**
- **Desperdício no download:** baixei por engano os arquivos estaduais de 2022 (cerca de 2,4 GB) e apaguei em seguida.
- **Chaves novas:** acrescentei chaves ao JSON sem mudar nenhuma já publicada. Estão anotadas em "Mudanças" no contrato: as duas variantes de cluster, a variação contra 2022, a troca de urna e o bloco de 2022.
- **Correções e conferências:** corrigi um bug na regra de tipo de local (presídio não era reconhecido pelo tipo oficial do TSE). Conferi as contagens por UF com SQL direto (AC 18, MT 50, RR 20 seções de 90%, igual ao JSON) e os modelos de Registro com awk.

**Para você rodar com a coleta completa:**
1. `cd apuracao &amp;&amp; bun run secoes -- --refazer-erros &amp;&amp; bun run secoes -- --so-conferir`
2. `python3 scripts/apuracao-2026-secoes.py`. Sem `--parcial`, o script recusa rodar enquanto houver seção pendente ou com erro, e acrescenta a conferência contra o total nacional (`final.json`).
3. `pytest -q tests/test_apuracao_2026_secoes.py`, depois commitar o JSON e o md.

O texto do memorando sai do JSON, então os números do md se atualizam sozinhos. A regeneração leva cerca de 35 segundos na base parcial.

alguns insights que tive que podem complementar:

1) Apuração mostrar por região mostrando claramente o efeito "votos do nordeste" lote a lote.
2) somar os votos de senadores do PL de todo o Brasil (ou de aliados do Flávio) vs votos do Flávio em todos os lugares e ver onde isso diverge mais., similar ao que fizemos com governadores.
3) melhorar uma tabela em anexo, não vi aí Raquel Lyra por exemplo. O que houve? 
4) na parte do erro do TSE, acho que seria interessante avaliar o sistema de centralização dos dados que, ao meu ver, faz importação num Oracle Exadata, o que pode engargalar num fluxo maior de dados devido a lentidão de imports em relação aos índices nessas tabelas e explicar a questão de arquitetura relacionada a isso na minha hipótese. É interessante falar sobre o comportamento de bancos de dados oracle com alto throughput de dados e dar uma recomendação ao TSE: criar um sistema de fila usando Kafka que vai produzindo dados para essa fila e criar um consumidor do outro lado que leve a uma importação mais "smooth", a mesma tática usada pela Uber para operar com o banco Cassandra e evitar problemas de alto throughput de dados. Outra recomendação é adotar um banco de dados distribuído, como HBase ou outras opções (analise o volume de dados para gerar recomendações)  em conjunto com um sistema de fila e mostre o desenho dessa arquitetura para mostrar o mundo ideal
5) Analise e escreva sobre a questão dos fechamentos de cada região, lugares que a eleição sempre termina muito tarde comparando com 2022, e isso é importante de mencionar e falar da importância dos fiscais de partido para evitar essas eleições terminando tarde assim, que também podem ser indício de mesário pianista, compra de votos, boca de urna etc. 
6) E também a apuração que sempre traz lentidão maior em certos estados e podemos comparar isso com 2022 e 2026 também. 

São esses os pontos que vi que podem ser trabalhados em paralelo enquanto vamos aguardando o coletor

<task-notification>
<task-id>aa554d0591cfa9e3e</task-id>
<tool-use-id>toolu_01HMfz5aWAC6dWntcqTocmfv</tool-use-id>

<summary>Agent "W11: arquitetura da totalização do TSE e recomendação" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The architecture block is in chapter 3 and pushed to main as `0a4c6d5` (data) and `af62fdf` (figures, text and tests). The main finding: in 2020 the TSE did run totalization on an Oracle database on Exadata. No document I could read says what ran totalization or publication in 2026. Leonardo's Exadata/index hypothesis is labelled as his hypothesis, unproven, everywhere it appears.

**What the documents prove about the TSE's infrastructure** (all listed with URL, date and what each proves in `analysis/apuracao_2026/fontes_arquitetura.json`):
- **2020 technical note** (TSE, 17/11/2020, PDF of 356,605 bytes, SHA-256 `abdcbc4e…cfd13`). In 2020 the TSE centralised totalization for the whole country for the first time. It ran on an Oracle database on an Exadata X8 Full Rack (8 nodes) plus a Half Rack (4 nodes) as reserve. The contract is TSE nº 22/2020, awarded without bidding. The tables received "more than one million rows per minute" from 17h. That year's slowdown came from the database's query plan, built while the tables were empty, and the totalization system was stopped to rebuild it. Only 2 of the 5 planned tests ran on the new machine.
- **Location and control:** the machines sit in a vault room at the TSE and are run by its own staff (Aos Fatos, 28/11/2020).
- **Later contracts:** the TSE's IT-contracts page lists an "expansion" of the Oracle Cloud at Customer contract (2022–2023 tab) and Oracle hardware support in 2025. The technical annex of that expansion could not be read because the site kept refusing requests.
- **Official pipeline:** the TSE's resolution says the receiving system (RecArquivos) hands the packages to the totalization system (Sistot). In 2022 the TSE itself described the arrival of ballot reports as "a bank queue" (ConJur, 29/10/2022).
- **2026:** the TSE president put the congestion in the publication system ("conversion of data by the publication program") and said totalization was not affected. IT isolated other systems to let the flow through. There is no technical report.
- **2022 and 2024:** I found no record of a comparable stoppage, though a search returning nothing does not prove there was none.

**A new finding from our own data.** Each section's public file carries the time the TSE recorded receiving its ballot report. In our section-by-section collection (190,223 sections, 20 states) that receipt time disappears in three windows that match the national file's stops:
- 18:00:55 to 18:08:47
- 18:49:46 to 18:54:03
- 19:31:50 to 19:59:22, which is the general pause

There are two possible readings, and the public files don't tell them apart. Either reception itself stopped, or the stamp is applied by a later component that stopped along with publication. Either way it wasn't only the shop window.

**Sizing.** The collection misses BA, MG, PE, PR, RJ, RS and SP, so the extrapolation (×2.62) gives an order of magnitude, not the national minute.

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| Ballot report size | 10.75 KB average; 5.4 GB for the whole country, 58 GB with urn logs |
| Vote rows (candidate × office) | 115 per section; 57.5 million for the night |
| Peak of the national file (18:43–18:49) | 5,163 sections/min, about 86 per second, about 9,900 vote rows per second |
| 2022, whole country | peak 5,022 reports/min; median 45 s from receipt to first totalization (90% within 93 s) |
| Files published in 2026 | peak 6,052 new versions/min, up to 262 MB/min, against about 55 MB/min of ballot reports coming in. These counts are a floor, because our collector doesn't read every file every minute |
| Minutes with no new file, 19:33–20:01 | 28 |
| Longest national stop | held back 100,614 sections, about 11.6 million rows |

The text states as inference that this is small for any modern database and that 2022 handled the same peak without stopping. So if there was a bottleneck, it was design, not capacity. The recommendation is sized to that: a write-once event log (Kafka or similar, split by state, with chained hashes), consumers that can't double-count a section, running totals instead of recounting the country each batch, and publication on a fixed timer. A distributed database is recommended only for running two data centres at once, not for volume. The TSE's peak is about 1% of what one Uber Cassandra cluster wrote in 2016.

The Uber sources are its engineering blog on Kafka (21/12/2020), the SIGMOD 2021 paper, and its Cassandra post (20/07/2023). For "a million writes per second" I could only find the High Scalability summary of a 2016 Uber talk, not an original Uber text, and that is how it is cited.

**The two figures:**
- `volume_noite`: bars per minute from 17h to 21h30, switchable between 2026 receipts (extrapolated), 2022 receipts (whole country) and 2026 published versions. The national file's stops are shaded and the pace of the national file is a gold line.
- `arquitetura_totalizacao`: two panels, "how the documents describe it today" (the author's hypothesis box dashed) and "how it could be". Every box has a tooltip with one sentence and its source. On phones it switches to a stacked HTML version with the same tooltips.

It has a home-style analogy box (the bakery cashier), the mechanism (index write amplification, locks on total rows, recomputing totals, synchronous publication), the recommendation box, what the TSE could publish, and the closing sentence about proof versus hypothesis. The data comes from `scripts/apuracao-2026-arquitetura.py`; the reasoning is written up in `analysis/apuracao_2026/arquitetura.md`.

## 1. A noite por região (capítulo 2, `h3` "A noite por região")
Reconstruí a contagem somando os 28 arquivos de UF, minuto a minuto, pela hora de geração do TSE. No fim, a soma bate com o arquivo nacional voto a voto.
- **Não houve virada.** Lula não esteve à frente em nenhum minuto.
- **A vantagem de Flávio:** em pontos, foi a 10,63 às 18:10, com 14,7% das seções; em votos, a 5,62 milhões às 19:14. Terminou em 1,87 ponto e 2,22 milhões.
- **Quem chegou a 90% das seções, e quando:** Sul 19:14, Centro-Oeste 19:16, Norte 20:10, Sudeste 20:16, Nordeste 20:51.
- **Quando o Nordeste passou a dominar:** às 19:27 ele já era a maior parte do que faltava apurar (45,9% de 22,03 milhões de válidos). A partir das 20:55, foi a maior região de todo lote com pelo menos 10 mil válidos.
- **Parcela de Lula dentro dos lotes:** entre 39,2% e 42,3% das 17:30 às 18:30; entre 56,5% e 76,0% das 21h às 23h.
- **Saldo final por região (Flávio menos Lula):** Nordeste −11,10 milhões, Sudeste +5,66, Sul +5,04, Centro-Oeste +2,17, Norte +0,46.
- **Por que a vantagem caiu (selo de inferência):** mantendo cada região no seu peso final nos válidos, a vantagem do pico seria 5,15 pontos, não 10,63. Logo, 62,5% da queda veio da ordem de chegada das regiões. O resto, 3,28 pontos, é a ordem de chegada dentro de cada região: no Sudeste, a margem de Flávio caiu de 16,7 para 11,7 pontos.
- **Correção da leitura simples:** no pico, Sul, Centro-Oeste e Norte pesavam acima do próprio tamanho, e o Sudeste estava atrasado junto com o Nordeste. O "efeito Nordeste" puro só aparece depois das 19:32, quando só o Nordeste seguia abaixo do peso (24,2% contra 28,2%).
- **O que o painel do TSE mostrava:** a versão das 18:48:59 era o retrato das 18:45 e, logo antes de ser trocada às 19:14, a soma das UFs já tinha 117.699 seções a mais. A das 19:14:08 era o retrato das 19:08 e ficou até 20:04:39; no último segundo tinha 56,6 minutos e estava 101.652 seções atrás.

## 2. Os estados lentos, em dois anos (capítulo 3, `h3` "Os estados lentos, em dois anos")
Em 2022, os dados abertos trazem duas horas por seção: o recebimento do boletim e a primeira entrada dele numa totalização parcial. Usei a segunda, que é a régua mais próxima de "totalizado"; o texto explica a diferença. Ela vem em mediana 45 segundos depois do recebimento.
- **2026 foi mais rápido.** O país passou de 50% das seções às 18:48 (19:35 em 2022), de 90% às 20:17 (20:30) e de 99% às 21:14 (22:14). As 27 UFs chegaram aos 99% mais cedo.
- **A lentidão é estrutural.** Doze UFs ficaram acima da mediana nos dois anos: AL, AM, BA, CE, MA, MG, PA, PE, PI, RJ, RN, SP. Só AC e SE trocaram de lado. A ordem das UFs se repete entre os anos (correlação de postos 0,90).
- **Fuso não explica.** O boletim de urna marca hora local (15:00 em AC e AM, 16:00 em MS, MT, RO e RR), mas as urnas fecharam às 17h de Brasília. As UFs fora de Brasília chegaram aos 99% em mediana às 20:11, antes das demais (20:59). Das seis, só o Amazonas está entre as lentas.
- **A cauda tem endereço fixo.** Do 99% ao 100% passaram 313 minutos no AM, 268 no MA e 189 em MG. Tabatinga fechou por último às 02:59 e já estava entre os cinco últimos municípios do AM em 2022. No país, a ordem de fechamento dos 5.570 municípios se repete com correlação de 0,65.
- **A pausa do TSE ficou separada da lentidão dos estados.** Nas 20 UFs com coleta de seções completa, nenhum boletim tem hora de recebimento entre 19:31:50 e 19:59:22; em 2022 a maior lacuna no mesmo intervalo foi de 23 segundos. AC, RO, RR e TO cruzaram 99% logo depois da pausa. As três UFs que chegaram aos 90% mais tarde que em 2022 (AP, MG, RJ) cruzaram a marca depois dela.
- **O último 1% das seções do país** levou 5 horas e 45 minutos e trouxe 1,24 milhão de válidos, 66,5% de Lula.

  | zona, tamanho e tipo de local | +2,76 (IC +2,58 a +2,95) | +5,4 |

  - Sobra 27% da inclinação bruta.
  - O controle de tamanho aumenta a diferença em vez de reduzir, porque dentro da zona a seção pequena vota mais em Lula: +2,7 pontos com até 199 aptos.
  - Spearman seção a seção dentro da UF: GO +0,21 é o maior; AP −0,12 o menor.
- **Número do W6:** `secoes.json` publicou 1.199 seções depois das 19h com +2,73 (o coordenador citou 1.194 e +2,72; não achei arquivo com esses valores). Nas mesmas 12 UFs, a mesma conta dá hoje +2,74 em 1.094 seções; o W6 contava também seções de UFs ainda em coleta. A diferença para o meu estimador (+4,74) é de método, não de dado:
  - comparar cada seção tardia só com as não tardias da zona leva de +2,74 a +3,11;
  - comparar os dois grupos inteiros, ponderando pela zona, leva a +4,74.
- **Explicações concorrentes:**
  - Fila: a seção tardia tem 40 votantes a mais que as demais da zona; o tamanho sozinho explica 10% da variação dentro da zona.
  - Identificação lenta: +0,7 a +1,1 ponto de habilitação por ano de nascimento. O boletim traz essas contagens para todas as seções; idade por seção não existe para o país. Com o mesmo tamanho, a seção tardia atendeu 2,4 votantes por hora a menos.
  - Irregularidade: não é testável com o boletim. O texto diz o que testaria (log da urna, ata, BO, representação).

**Figuras e texto**
- Quatro figuras no `CATALOGO_FIGURAS.md`: `fechamento_regioes` e `fechamento_persistencia` em `pagina_fig_fechamento.py`; `fechamento_voto_lula` e `fechamento_voto_zona` em `pagina_fig_fechamento_b.py`.
- O texto (`pagina_texto_fechamento.py`) entra no fim do capítulo 12 como h3 "Onde a votação termina tarde, em dois anos", com figura antes do parágrafo e réguas bruta e controlada no mesmo destaque.
- A frase sobre fiscais de partido é juízo editorial declarado. Pianista, compra de voto e boca de urna estão num quadro de hipótese, com a prova que cada um exigiria.
- Arquivos compartilhados: só acrescentei linhas em `pagina_figuras.py`, `pagina_view.py` (script, JSON e reprodução), `pagina_cap_c.py` e no catálogo. Não precisei mexer na contagem do teste de figuras, que já virou `&gt;= 43`.
